Después de revisar los cuatro con más detalle, **cambiaría el criterio de selección** respecto a la búsqueda general anterior. Para este mini-project no premiaría al dataset que tenga más filas ni al que permita obtener el F1 más alto; premiaría al que permita construir una historia metodológica coherente con **Machine Learning + dinámica de procesos + SCADA/control**, usando correctamente validación, feature engineering y varios modelos del curso.\\\\n\\\\nDel syllabus de DS675 hay cuatro ideas particularmente relevantes: el mini-project es un **proyecto de implementación en ML**, los outcomes asociados incluyen **evaluar recursos online, reconocer problemas apropiados para ML y aplicar algoritmos en situaciones nuevas**, el curso cubre desde KNN/linear regression hasta árboles, ensembles, dimensionality reduction y RNN, y el syllabus incluso menciona que una contribución novedosa significativa puede distinguir un proyecto excepcional. Por eso voy a penalizar cualquier formulación que termine siendo simplemente “corrí un LSTM sobre un benchmark de anomaly detection”.\\\\n\\\\n## Resultado general\\\\n\\\\nMi ranking para **este mini-project específico** sería:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n\\\\\\\\textbf{1. SWaT}\\\\n>\\\\n\\\\\\\\textbf{2. BATADAL}\\\\n>\\\\n\\\\\\\\textbf{3. WADI}\\\\n>\\\\n\\\\\\\\textbf{4. SKAB}\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nPero hay un matiz importante:\\\\n\\\\n- **SWaT** = mejor balance general.\\\\n- **BATADAL** = probablemente el más limpio para hacer *dynamic modeling / system identification con ML* dentro del alcance del curso.\\\\n- **WADI** = potencial técnico incluso mayor que SWaT, pero bastante más riesgo y trabajo.\\\\n- **SKAB** = muy atractivo físicamente, pero más fuerte como benchmark de anomaly/fault detection que como problema de control/modelado entrada–salida.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# 1. Matriz comparativa\\\\n\\\\nHe usado criterios que considero especialmente relevantes para DS675.\\\\n\\\\n| Criterio | Peso | SWaT | BATADAL | WADI | SKAB |\\\\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\\\\n| Claridad del problema supervisado | 15% | **5** | **5** | **5** | 4 |\\\\n| Relevancia SCADA/control/dinámica | 15% | **5** | 4 | **5** | 3.5 |\\\\n| Compatibilidad con modelos DS675 | 15% | **5** | **5** | **5** | 5 |\\\\n| Validación temporal manejable | 10% | 4 | **5** | 3.5 | 3 |\\\\n| Cantidad efectiva de datos | 10% | **5** | 4 | **5** | 2.5 |\\\\n| Interpretabilidad física | 10% | **5** | **5** | 4 | **5** |\\\\n| Originalidad disponible | 10% | 4 | 4.5 | **5** | 4 |\\\\n| Alcance manejable en un semestre | 10% | 4 | **5** | 3 | **5** |\\\\n| Trabajo Kaggle existente útil | 5% | 4 | 3 | 3 | **4** |\\\\n| **Resultado aproximado** | | **93/100** | **91/100** | **89/100** | **81/100** |\\\\n\\\\nLa diferencia entre los tres primeros es suficientemente pequeña como para que **la formulación del problema** importe más que dos puntos de score.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# 🥇 1. SWaT\\\\n\\\\n## Qué es realmente\\\\n\\\\nSWaT —Secure Water Treatment— es un testbed físico que replica una planta de tratamiento de agua en **seis etapas**, incluyendo raw-water storage, chemical dosing, ultrafiltration, dechlorination, reverse osmosis y backwash. Cada sección incluye sensores, actuadores y PLCs, de modo que realmente existe una estructura de feedback entre medidas físicas y acciones de control. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"0\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEl dataset clásico contiene **11 días continuos**: siete de operación normal y cuatro con ataques, con valores de **51 sensores y actuadores**. La referencia oficial de iTrust informa 41 ataques en esos cuatro días. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"1\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nUna caracterización publicada reporta aproximadamente **946,722 observaciones a 1 Hz**, con 25 variables de sensores y 26 estados de actuadores. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"2\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es importante porque tenemos algo que se parece genuinamente a:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nu(t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{proceso físico}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\ny(t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nPLC\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nu(t+1)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nNo tenemos que inventar la interpretación de control.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Lo que NO haría\\\\n\\\\nLa formulación más obvia sería:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nX_t\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Normal/Attack}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ncon Random Forest, XGBoost o LSTM.\\\\n\\\\nFunciona.\\\\n\\\\nPero para tus intereses me parece una **oportunidad desperdiciada**.\\\\n\\\\nSWaT ya está enormemente estudiado como benchmark de anomaly/intrusion detection. Existe mucha literatura con autoencoders, LSTM, GRU, CNN, ConvLSTM y variantes profundas. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"3\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEl mirror de Kaggle que revisé tiene actualmente **10 notebooks, 1 discusión y más de 2,200 descargas**, así que Kaggle no está completamente saturado, pero académicamente SWaT sí es un benchmark muy conocido. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"4\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# La formulación que recomiendo\\\\n\\\\n## Dynamic process prediction\\\\n\\\\nTomaría **una sección física concreta**, no las 51 variables completas inicialmente.\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo, un tanque:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n[u(t-k:t),y(t-k:t)]\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nL(t+h)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ndonde:\\\\n\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(u\\\\\\\\) = estados de pumps/valves,\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(y\\\\\\\\) = level, flow, pressure, etc.,\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(L(t+h)\\\\\\\\) = nivel futuro del tanque.\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo conceptualmente:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\{\\\\nMV101,\\\\\\\\,\\\\nP101,\\\\\\\\,\\\\nP102,\\\\\\\\,\\\\nFIT101,\\\\\\\\,\\\\nLIT101(t-k:t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nLIT101(t+30s)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEsto ya parece **black-box dynamic system modeling**.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# El experimento central que haría\\\\n\\\\nCompararía tres representaciones.\\\\n\\\\n### Modelo 1 — Static\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nX_t\\\\\\\\rightarrow y_{t+h}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n### Modelo 2 — Dynamic\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n[X_t,X_{t-1},...,X_{t-k}]\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow y_{t+h}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n### Modelo 3 — Dynamic + engineered features\\\\n\\\\nAgregar:\\\\n\\\\n- rolling mean,\\\\n- rolling std,\\\\n- rate of change,\\\\n- valve/pump transition indicators,\\\\n- accumulated pump-on time.\\\\n\\\\nY comparar:\\\\n\\\\n- Linear/Ridge Regression\\\\n- KNN Regressor\\\\n- Decision Tree\\\\n- Random Forest\\\\n- Gradient Boosting\\\\n- opcional RNN al final.\\\\n\\\\nEso conecta de manera casi perfecta con el curriculum.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Lo realmente interesante\\\\n\\\\nDespués de modelar operación normal:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\hat y(t+h)=f(X_{t-k:t})\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\npodrías calcular:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nr(t)=\\\\ny(t)-\\\\\\\\hat y(t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny estudiar si durante ataques:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n|r(t)|\\\\\\\\uparrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEs decir:\\\\n\\\\n### ML dynamic model → residual → anomaly indicator\\\\n\\\\nEso conecta tu background de control con ML sin convertir el proyecto en control clásico.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Validación correcta\\\\n\\\\nAquí hay un peligro enorme.\\\\n\\\\n**Random train/test split sería metodológicamente incorrecto.**\\\\n\\\\nA 1 Hz:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nx_t\\\\n\\\\\\\\quad\\\\\\\\text{y}\\\\\\\\quad\\\\nx_{t+1}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nson casi la misma condición física.\\\\n\\\\nSi uno termina en train y el otro en test, el desempeño parece artificialmente excelente.\\\\n\\\\nYo usaría:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Train}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Validation}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Test}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ncomo bloques cronológicos.\\\\n\\\\nAdemás añadiría un **gap** igual o mayor que la largest lag window.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Otro baseline imprescindible\\\\n\\\\nPara level/temperature/pressure:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n\\\\\\\\hat y(t+h)=y(t)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nPersistence model.\\\\n\\\\nPorque si el tanque cambia lentamente, un Random Forest con \\\\\\\\(R^2=0.999\\\\\\\\) puede parecer impresionante cuando simplemente decir:\\\\n\\\\n> “dentro de 10 segundos será casi igual que ahora”\\\\n\\\\nobtiene \\\\\\\\(R^2=0.998\\\\\\\\).\\\\n\\\\nEste baseline sería una de las cosas que haría tu trabajo mucho más riguroso.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Pros de SWaT\\\\n\\\\n- Testbed **físico**, no simplemente simulación.\\\\n- PLC + actuadores + sensores.\\\\n- Variables con significado físico.\\\\n- Múltiples stages.\\\\n- 1 Hz.\\\\n- Casi un millón de muestras.\\\\n- Targets claros de regression.\\\\n- Target alternativo para classification.\\\\n- Excelente para lag features.\\\\n- Excelente para nonlinear models.\\\\n- Permite Linear → KNN → RF → Boosting → RNN.\\\\n- Permite estudiar operación normal y abnormal.\\\\n- Excelente portfolio industrial.\\\\n- Existe trabajo previo suficiente para hacer revisión crítica. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"5\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n## Contras\\\\n\\\\n- Academic benchmark bastante explotado.\\\\n- Alta autocorrelación.\\\\n- Random split produce leakage.\\\\n- Attack classification puede convertirse en un proyecto genérico de cybersecurity.\\\\n- 51 variables pueden distraer del objetivo.\\\\n- Algunos ataques manipulan directamente sensores/actuadores; hay que decidir si queremos **detect**, **predict** o **model the process**.\\\\n- Million-row dataset no significa million independent observations.\\\\n- Requiere entender la topología del proceso antes de elegir features.\\\\n\\\\n### Evaluación\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\\\\\textbf{93/100}}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n**Mi #1.**\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# 🥈 2. BATADAL\\\\n\\\\nBATADAL se vuelve muchísimo más interesante cuando lo miramos desde **system modeling** y no únicamente desde cybersecurity.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Sistema físico representado\\\\n\\\\nBATADAL modela la red de agua **C-Town**.\\\\n\\\\nIncluye:\\\\n\\\\n- 1 reservoir,\\\\n- **7 tanks**,\\\\n- **11 pumps**,\\\\n- valves,\\\\n- cientos de pipes/junctions,\\\\n- PLCs,\\\\n- SCADA central.\\\\n\\\\nLa red completa incluye 388 junctions y 429 pipes. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"6\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nLos datos SCADA publicados contienen **43 variables** relacionadas con:\\\\n\\\\n- tank levels,\\\\n- pump states,\\\\n- pump flows,\\\\n- valve state/flow,\\\\n- pressures. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"7\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nEsto genera relaciones físicamente muy claras:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Pump status}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Flow}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Tank level}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Pressure}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Dataset structure\\\\n\\\\nAquí BATADAL tiene algo que me gusta muchísimo.\\\\n\\\\nHay tres datasets temporales claramente separados:\\\\n\\\\n### Training 1\\\\n\\\\nAproximadamente **un año completo** de normal operation.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n8761 \\\\\\\\text{ hourly observations}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n### Training 2\\\\n\\\\nAproximadamente seis meses:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n4177 \\\\\\\\text{ observations}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ncon ataques.\\\\n\\\\n### Test\\\\n\\\\nAproximadamente tres meses:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n2089 \\\\\\\\text{ observations}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ntambién con ataques.\\\\n\\\\nLos datos SCADA están muestreados **cada hora**. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"8\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Aquí está su gran ventaja\\\\n\\\\nPara DS675, BATADAL es probablemente **el dataset conceptualmente más limpio de los cuatro**.\\\\n\\\\nPodemos plantear, por ejemplo:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\nX_{t-24:t}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nL_{T1}(t+1)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ndonde:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nX=\\\\n\\\\\\\\{\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{pump statuses},\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{pump flows},\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{pressures},\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{tank levels}\\\\n\\\\\\\\}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\ny=L_{T1}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nuna hora en el futuro.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Dinámica particularmente interesante\\\\n\\\\nComo hay ciclos de consumo diarios, podemos probar lags:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n1,\\\\\\\\;2,\\\\\\\\;3,\\\\\\\\;6,\\\\\\\\;12,\\\\\\\\;24\\\\\\\\;\\\\\\\\text{hours}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nIncluso:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\ny(t-24)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\npuede ser muy informativo.\\\\n\\\\nEso produce una oportunidad preciosa para estudiar:\\\\n\\\\n### Short-term memory\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nt-1:t\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nvs.\\\\n\\\\n### Daily operational memory\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nt-24:t\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Un proyecto muy elegante\\\\n\\\\n## Phase 1 — Model normal hydraulic behavior\\\\n\\\\nTrain Dataset 1:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nf(X_{t-k:t})\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\ny_{t+1}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\n## Phase 2 — Test prediction\\\\n\\\\nnormal unseen data.\\\\n\\\\n## Phase 3 — Attack robustness\\\\n\\\\nAplicar el modelo sobre Training Dataset 2.\\\\n\\\\nCalcular:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nr(t)=y(t)-\\\\\\\\hat y(t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny comprobar si los ataques producen residual distributions diferentes.\\\\n\\\\nEso tiene muchísimo más sentido ingenieril que hacer:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nX_t\\\\\\\\rightarrow ATTACK\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ndirectamente.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# ¿Por qué no es #1?\\\\n\\\\nPor dos razones.\\\\n\\\\n## 1. Es una simulación\\\\n\\\\nBATADAL utiliza **EPANET** para simular el comportamiento hidráulico de C-Town y los ataques. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"9\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEso no lo convierte en un mal dataset.\\\\n\\\\nEPANET proporciona una dinámica hidráulica físicamente coherente.\\\\n\\\\nPero SWaT/WADI provienen de testbeds físicos reales.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## 2. Resolución temporal de una hora\\\\n\\\\nÉsta es la desventaja más importante.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\Delta t=1h\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nPor tanto, estamos modelando:\\\\n\\\\n- operating cycles,\\\\n- demand,\\\\n- storage dynamics,\\\\n\\\\nmás que los **transitorios rápidos de un loop de control**.\\\\n\\\\nNo vas a observar detalladamente:\\\\n\\\\n> pump turns on → pressure transient → controller response.\\\\n\\\\nEn cambio SWaT sí ofrece 1 Hz.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Pero para un mini-project eso puede ser positivo\\\\n\\\\n8761 filas son pocas comparadas con SWaT.\\\\n\\\\nPero no son pocas para:\\\\n\\\\n- Linear Regression,\\\\n- Ridge,\\\\n- KNN,\\\\n- Decision Trees,\\\\n- Random Forest,\\\\n- Gradient Boosting.\\\\n\\\\nY entrenar modelos será extremadamente fácil.\\\\n\\\\nPuedes hacer 50 experimentos sin necesitar GPU.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Leakage\\\\n\\\\nAquí el problema es mucho más manejable.\\\\n\\\\nNo random split.\\\\n\\\\nSimplemente:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\begin{aligned}\\\\nTrain &: months\\\\\\\\,1-8\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nValidation &: months\\\\\\\\,9-10\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nTest &: months\\\\\\\\,11-12\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{aligned}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nPor ejemplo.\\\\n\\\\nLos splits temporales son muy naturales.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Existing work\\\\n\\\\nBATADAL fue originalmente una **competition de attack detection**, e incluso el ganador utilizó conocimiento hidráulico del sistema combinado con anomaly detection. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"10\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es interesante porque podemos hacer algo diferente:\\\\n\\\\n> No intentar ganar nuevamente BATADAL.\\\\n\\\\nSino estudiar:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Can conventional ML learn the normal dynamic input-output behavior of C-Town?}\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEn Kaggle, el mirror que encontré tiene sólo **4 notebooks y 1 discussion**, considerablemente menos actividad que muchos benchmarks clásicos. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"11\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Pros BATADAL\\\\n\\\\n- SCADA puro.\\\\n- Tank/pump/pressure/flow interpretation excelente.\\\\n- Features documentadas.\\\\n- Target muy claro.\\\\n- La topología está disponible.\\\\n- Separación temporal excelente.\\\\n- Computacionalmente trivial.\\\\n- Ideal para Linear/KNN/Tree/RF/Boosting.\\\\n- Lags de 24 h tienen interpretación física.\\\\n- Muy poco trabajo Kaggle relativo.\\\\n- Muy fácil presentar el proyecto.\\\\n- Muy fácil hacer gráficos e interpretar resultados.\\\\n- Originalidad razonable si abandonamos attack classification.\\\\n- Train normal + attack dataset permite residual-based analysis.\\\\n\\\\n## Contras\\\\n\\\\n- Simulado.\\\\n- Sampling de 1 hora.\\\\n- Sólo ~15k observaciones totales.\\\\n- No representa bien transitorios rápidos de control.\\\\n- Apenas siete attack events en algunos subsets: muchos attack-labeled rows no equivalen a muchos ataques independientes. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"12\\\\\\\"}\\\\n- Si el proyecto se convierte en supervised attack classification, el tamaño efectivo de la clase minoritaria es pequeño.\\\\n- Al usar 24 lags × muchas variables, dimensionality puede crecer muy rápido.\\\\n\\\\n### Evaluación\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\\\\\textbf{91/100}}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nY hay algo importante:\\\\n\\\\n> **Para un mini-project que quieres terminar impecablemente, BATADAL puede ser incluso más seguro que SWaT.**\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# 🥉 3. WADI\\\\n\\\\n## Qué representa\\\\n\\\\nWADI es la pareja natural de SWaT.\\\\n\\\\nMientras SWaT trata **water treatment**, WADI representa **water distribution**.\\\\n\\\\nTiene tres stages:\\\\n\\\\n### P1 — Primary\\\\n\\\\nDos raw-water tanks de aproximadamente 2500 L.\\\\n\\\\n### P2 — Distribution\\\\n\\\\nDos elevated reservoirs que alimentan **seis consumer tanks**.\\\\n\\\\n### P3 — Return\\\\n\\\\nUnused water regresa y puede recircularse hacia P1. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"13\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEsto es tremendamente interesante desde control porque aparecen:\\\\n\\\\n- storage,\\\\n- consumer demand,\\\\n- pumps,\\\\n- reservoirs,\\\\n- return flow,\\\\n- pressure,\\\\n- level,\\\\n- valves.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Dataset\\\\n\\\\nLa referencia oficial registra:\\\\n\\\\n- **16 días continuos**\\\\n- 14 normales\\\\n- 2 con ataques\\\\n- **123 sensores y actuadores**\\\\n- 15 ataques. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"14\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nFuentes posteriores describen 69 sensores + 54 actuadores y muestreo de **1 segundo**. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"15\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEstamos nuevamente alrededor de un millón de observaciones.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Por qué técnicamente podría ser el #1\\\\n\\\\nWADI posiblemente tiene la dinámica más interesante de los cuatro.\\\\n\\\\nEn una red de distribución:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Demand}\\\\n+\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Pump operation}\\\\n+\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Storage}\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{Levels/flows/pressures}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny estas relaciones cambian dependiendo del operating regime.\\\\n\\\\nPodríamos hacer algo como:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n[\\\\nPumps_{t-k:t},\\\\nFlows_{t-k:t},\\\\nLevels_{t-k:t},\\\\nPressures_{t-k:t}\\\\n]\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nLevel_{ER1}(t+h)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\no:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow P_{consumer}(t+h)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nEso es prácticamente:\\\\n\\\\n### SCADA data-driven dynamic modeling.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Incluso más interesante\\\\n\\\\nComparar diferentes horizons:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nh=\\\\n10s,\\\\\\\\;30s,\\\\\\\\;60s,\\\\\\\\;5min\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ny observar:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nRMSE(h)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nA medida que aumenta \\\\\\\\(h\\\\\\\\), debería hacerse más difícil.\\\\n\\\\nEso es una pregunta científica clara.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Entonces, ¿por qué sólo #3?\\\\n\\\\nPor **riesgo de ejecución**.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Problema 1 — Dimensionalidad\\\\n\\\\n123 signals.\\\\n\\\\nNo quieres terminar con:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n123\\\\\\\\times60\\\\n=\\\\n7380\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nfeatures porque creaste 60 lags.\\\\n\\\\nEso sería un proyecto de preprocessing.\\\\n\\\\nTendríamos que elegir un **subsystem físico**.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Problema 2 — Dataset cleaning\\\\n\\\\nLa propia página oficial de iTrust advierte que ciertas secciones del plant run fueron inestables y que posteriormente tuvieron que eliminar lecturas afectadas y publicar archivos corregidos. También hubo correcciones a los labels de ataques. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"16\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nNo es necesariamente malo.\\\\n\\\\nPero es algo que tendremos que documentar.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Problema 3 — Gaps\\\\n\\\\nTrabajos recientes que usan WADI señalan incluso un largo gap en la secuencia temporal de la versión A2. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"17\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nUn lag generator ingenuo puede crear:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nx_{t-1}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\na través de un gap temporal que en realidad dura días.\\\\n\\\\nEso sería incorrecto.\\\\n\\\\nHay que segmentar las series antes.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Problema 4 — menos soporte Kaggle\\\\n\\\\nEl mirror actual que encontré tiene:\\\\n\\\\n- **4 notebooks**\\\\n- **0 discussions**\\\\n- aproximadamente 947 downloads. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"18\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nEs excelente para originalidad.\\\\n\\\\nPero menos conveniente para el outcome del curso de **evaluar críticamente recursos existentes**.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n# Gran oportunidad original\\\\n\\\\nPrecisamente porque casi todo el trabajo WADI existente está orientado a attack/anomaly detection —con GNN, autoencoders, LSTM-VAE, etc.— hay bastante espacio para:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n\\\\\\\\text{system prediction instead of anomaly classification}\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nWADI continúa siendo un benchmark activo de anomaly detection. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"19\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Pros WADI\\\\n\\\\n- Testbed físico.\\\\n- SCADA/PLC.\\\\n- Pumps + valves + storage + consumers.\\\\n- 123 variables.\\\\n- 1 Hz.\\\\n- Muchísimos datos.\\\\n- Dinámica multivariable.\\\\n- Nonlinearity.\\\\n- Operating regimes.\\\\n- Demand effects.\\\\n- Excelente para state/output prediction.\\\\n- Excelente para residual-based fault/attack detection.\\\\n- Poca actividad Kaggle → mucha originalidad.\\\\n- Excelente portfolio.\\\\n- Quizás el sistema más parecido a SCADA municipal real de los cuatro.\\\\n\\\\n## Contras\\\\n\\\\n- Dataset grande.\\\\n- 123 variables pueden complicar muchísimo feature engineering.\\\\n- Correcciones oficiales del dataset.\\\\n- Gaps y unstable sections.\\\\n- 1.2M filas muy autocorrelacionadas.\\\\n- Sólo 14 días normales.\\\\n- 15 attack events es poco a nivel de eventos.\\\\n- Si no restringimos el scope, se convierte fácilmente en un proyecto demasiado grande.\\\\n- Mapping de los tags y subsystems requiere más trabajo que SWaT o BATADAL.\\\\n\\\\n### Evaluación\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\\\\\textbf{89/100}}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\nTécnicamente es extraordinario.\\\\n\\\\nPero para un mini-project:\\\\n\\\\n> **WADI tiene más ceiling Los sensores se muestrearon a **100 Hz** mientras las concentraciones cambiaban a intervalos aleatorios de aproximadamente 80–120 segundos. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"15\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEso es casi exactamente lo que queremos cuando hablamos de un **sistema dinámico no lineal con memoria**: la concentración cambia y los sensores no responden instantáneamente.\\\\n\\\\n### Problema supervisado recomendado\\\\n\\\\nNo usaría simplemente:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\nS(t)\\\\\\\\rightarrow C(t)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ncomo único experimento.\\\\n\\\\nPropondría:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n\\\\\\\\boxed{\\\\n[S(t),S(t-1),...,S(t-L)]\\\\n\\\\\\\\rightarrow\\\\nC_{CO}(t)\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\ncomo **dynamic soft sensor**.\\\\n\\\\nUna extensión:\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\[\\\\n[S(t),...,S(t-L)]\\\\\\\\rightarrow C_{CO}(t+h)\\\\n\\\\\\\\]\\\\n\\\\npara estudiar distintos horizons.\\\\n\\\\n### X\\\\n\\\\nLos 16 canales de sensores químicos, con:\\\\n\\\\n- valores actuales,\\\\n- lags,\\\\n- diferencias \\\\\\\\(S_t-S_{t-k}\\\\\\\\),\\\\n- rolling means,\\\\n- rolling slopes.\\\\n\\\\n### y\\\\n\\\\nElegiría inicialmente:\\\\n\\\\n**CO concentration [ppm]** en `ethylene_CO`.\\\\n\\\\nDespués podría repetirse con ethylene.\\\\n\\\\nNo recomiendo empezar con multi-output porque complica el análisis sin aumentar necesariamente el valor del Milestone 1.\\\\n\\\\n### ¿Lags?\\\\n\\\\n**Sí, absolutamente.**\\\\n\\\\nLa propia motivación del dataset es que los sensores tienen respuesta lenta frente a cambios rápidos de concentración. El dataset fue publicado para investigar continuous monitoring y mejorar response time. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"16\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nAquí la pregunta científica natural es:\\\\n\\\\n> ¿Cuánto mejora la estimación de concentración cuando el modelo conoce el historial reciente de los sensores?\\\\n\\\\nEso es excelente para DS675.\\\\n\\\\n### Horizon\\\\n\\\\nPara estimación de concentración:\\\\n\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(h=0\\\\\\\\): soft sensing actual.\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(h=1\\\\\\\\) s.\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(h=5\\\\\\\\) s.\\\\n- \\\\\\\\(h=10\\\\\\\\) s.\\\\n\\\\nNo comenzaría con horizontes enormes.\\\\n\\\\n### Modelos\\\\n\\\\nUna secuencia muy adecuada al curso:\\\\n\\\\n1. Linear / Ridge Regression.\\\\n2. KNN Regression.\\\\n3. Random Forest.\\\\n4. Gradient Boosting / XGBoost si se permite.\\\\n5. SVR en un subset.\\\\n6. Opcional: MLP/LSTM al final.\\\\n\\\\n### Split correcto\\\\n\\\\n**Nunca random row split.**\\\\n\\\\nA 100 Hz, dos filas consecutivas están separadas sólo 0.01 s.\\\\n\\\\nUn random split permitiría que una observación a \\\\\\\\(t\\\\\\\\) quede en train y la de \\\\\\\\(t+0.01\\\\\\\\) en test.\\\\n\\\\nEso produciría un test artificialmente fácil.\\\\n\\\\nUsaría bloques cronológicos:\\\\n\\\\n**Train:** first 60%  \\\\n**Validation:** next 20%  \\\\n**Test:** last 20%\\\\n\\\\ny colocaría un **gap** de al menos el tamaño máximo de las lag windows.\\\\n\\\\n### Kaggle activity\\\\n\\\\nTiene aproximadamente **9 notebooks, 1 discussion y ~2,800 downloads**: suficiente actividad para revisar trabajos existentes, pero muy lejos del nivel de saturación de Titanic, C-MAPSS o Electric Motor Temperature. :chatgpt-content-reference{index=\\\\\\\"17\\\\\\\"}\\\\n\\\\nEste es exactamente el tipo de actividad intermedia que buscabas.\\\\n\\\\n### Debilidad habitual\\\\n\\\\nLa mayor amenaza metodológica es evaluar los millones de filas como si fueran observaciones IID.\\\\n\\\\nNo lo son.\\\\n\\\\nLa muestra independiente efectiva es muchísimo menor debido a la autocorrelación temporal.\\\\n\\\\n### Tres extensiones originales\\\\n\\\\n1. **Static vs dynamic representation:** current sensors vs sensors + history.\\\\n2. **Lag-window sensitivity:** 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10 seconds.\\\\n3. **Prediction horizon sensitivity:** \\\\\\\\(h=0,1,5,10\\\\\\\\) sec.\\\\n4. Versión académica en inglés de tu justificación, Value: 2\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t976 text 2. Versión académica en inglés de tu justificación\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t977 text Este texto conserva tu razonamiento, pero evita afirmaciones hidráulicas que todavía tendrían que demostrarse experimentalmente.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t978 container\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t979 text Writing\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t980 button Copy\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t981 button Open editor\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t982 text entry area (settable) Description: Start writing, Value: Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1\\n\\nMy selection of Area C was guided by an engineering assessment of the available hydraulic measurements and operating conditions across the three sectors of the L-Town network.\\n\\nArea A initially appeared attractive because it contains the largest number of pressure measurements. However, its supply passes through two pressure-reducing valves (PRVs), and its pressure behavior can be influenced by demand variations, seasonal consumption patterns, and unpredictable changes in water use. Area B also has a regulated supply boundary but offers only one measured pressure node, limiting the possibility of studying relationships between multiple pressure measurements.\\n\\nArea C presented a different and particularly interesting configuration. Rather than being supplied directly through a pressure-regulating valve, it receives water from tank T1, whose level varies with the balance between pump inflow and sector consumption. The available measurements include tank level, pump flow, and three pressure sensors (n1, n4, and n31). This configuration motivated my hypothesis that the hydraulic state of the tank, together with pressure measurements at nearby nodes, might provide useful information for forecasting future pressure.\\n\\nI selected n1 as the target because it has both the highest elevation and the longest pipe-path distance from T1 among the three monitored pressure nodes in Area C. These characteristics make it an interesting candidate for studying pressure sensitivity, although they do not independently establish it as the hydraulically most critical node in the sector.\\n\\nI also included AMR_n1, the local water-consumption measurement, to investigate whether demand information contributes predictive value beyond pressure history and the other hydraulic signals.\\n\\nThis approach builds on my previous research experience in dynamic pressure regulation in real water distribution networks (Arango-Martinez et al., 2026). In that work, pressure and flow measurements supported dynamic system identification and pressure-control design at a remote critical node. My earlier experience also motivated the use of superimposed daily time-series profiles and their mean curves to examine recurring patterns and operational variability.\\n\\nThe contribution proposed here is not a new forecasting algorithm, but a controlled investigation of whether physically motivated measurements provide incremental predictive information for 15-minute-ahead pressure forecasting. The experiments will test this hypothesis rather than assume that the additional variables necessarily improve prediction.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t983 heading Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1, Value: 3\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t984 text Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t985 text My selection of Area C was guided by an engineering assessment of the available hydraulic measurements and operating conditions across the three sectors of the L-Town network.\\n\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t\\t986 text Area A initially appeared attractive because it contains the largest number of pressure measurements. Versión académica en inglés de tu justificación\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t977 text Este texto conserva tu razonamiento, pero evita afirmaciones hidráulicas que todavía tendrían que demostrarse experimentalmente.\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t978 container\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t979 text Writing\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t980 button Copy\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t981 button Open editor\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t982 text entry area (settable) Description: Start writing, Value: Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1\n\nMy selection of Area C was guided by an engineering assessment of the available hydraulic measurements and operating conditions across the three sectors of the L-Town network.\n\nArea A initially appeared attractive because it contains the largest number of pressure measurements. However, its supply passes through two pressure-reducing valves (PRVs), and its pressure behavior can be influenced by demand variations, seasonal consumption patterns, and unpredictable changes in water use. Area B also has a regulated supply boundary but offers only one measured pressure node, limiting the possibility of studying relationships between multiple pressure measurements.\n\nArea C presented a different and particularly interesting configuration. Rather than being supplied directly through a pressure-regulating valve, it receives water from tank T1, whose level varies with the balance between pump inflow and sector consumption. The available measurements include tank level, pump flow, and three pressure sensors (n1, n4, and n31). This configuration motivated my hypothesis that the hydraulic state of the tank, together with pressure measurements at nearby nodes, might provide useful information for forecasting future pressure.\n\nI selected n1 as the target because it has both the highest elevation and the longest pipe-path distance from T1 among the three monitored pressure nodes in Area C. These characteristics make it an interesting candidate for studying pressure sensitivity, although they do not independently establish it as the hydraulically most critical node in the sector.\n\nI also included AMR_n1, the local water-consumption measurement, to investigate whether demand information contributes predictive value beyond pressure history and the other hydraulic signals.\n\nThis approach builds on my previous research experience in dynamic pressure regulation in real water distribution networks (Arango-Martinez et al., 2026). In that work, pressure and flow measurements supported dynamic system identification and pressure-control design at a remote critical node. My earlier experience also motivated the use of superimposed daily time-series profiles and their mean curves to examine recurring patterns and operational variability.\n\nThe contribution proposed here is not a new forecasting algorithm, but a controlled investigation of whether physically motivated measurements provide incremental predictive information for 15-minute-ahead pressure forecasting. The experiments will test this hypothesis rather than assume that the additional variables necessarily improve prediction.\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t983 heading Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1, Value: 3\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t984 text Engineering Rationale for Selecting Area C and Target Node n1\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t985 text My selection of Area C was guided by an engineering assessment of the available hydraulic measurements and operating conditions across the three sectors of the L-Town network.\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t986 text Area A initially appeared attractive because it contains the largest number of pressure measurements.         'conversation_id':cid,'title':raw['thread']['title'],'classification':classification,
        'classification_reason':reason,'completeness':'PARTIAL; official export not supplied',
        'source_file':src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
        'source_kind':'read_thread retrieval response; not an official ChatGPT export',
        'ordering':'ascending source turn startedAt; items retain source order within each turn',
        'timestamp_warning':'Turn times are preserved; individual message creation times are not supplied by this retrieval schema.',
        'browser_source_file':browser_path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix() if browser else None,
        'browser_warning':'DOM capture is a rendered view; exact original Markdown, attachments, hidden tool records and all branches are not established.',
        'conversation_created_at_raw':raw['thread'].get('createdAt'),
        'conversation_updated_at_raw':raw['thread'].get('updatedAt')
    },'turns':[t for _,t in turns],'attachments_as_returned':raw.get('attachments',[])}
    if browser: payload['browser_units_as_captured']=browser_units
    stem=ROOT/classification/cid
    dump(stem.with_suffix('.json'),payload)
    md=['# '+raw['thread']['title'],'','**PARTIAL RECOVERY — not an official export.**','',
        '- Conversation ID: '+cid,'- Classification: '+classification,
        '- Source JSON: '+src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
        '- Original conversation createdAt: '+str(raw['thread'].get('createdAt')),
        '- Original conversation updatedAt: '+str(raw['thread'].get('updatedAt')),
        '- Source response reports hasMore: '+str(raw.get('page',{}).get('hasMore')),
        '- Completeness of all historical messages and branches is unverified.',
        '- Headings and notices are archive annotations. Fenced message bodies below are copied programmatically without translation or cleanup.',
        '- Source attachment markers and content-reference placeholders are retained literally. They are not recovered message or attachment contents.',
        '- Times below belong to turns, not independently verified message creation timestamps.','',
        '## Retrieved records in chronological turn order','']
    localtimes=[]; relevant_messages=[]
    for n,(_,t) in enumerate(turns,1):
        start=t.get('startedAt');end=t.get('completedAt')
        if start:alltimes.append(start);localtimes.append(start)
        md+=['### Turn '+str(n),'','- Turn ID: '+str(t.get('id')),'- startedAt: '+str(start)+' ('+iso(start)+')','- completedAt: '+str(end)+' ('+iso(end)+')','']
        for j,item in enumerate(t.get('items',[])):
            items_total+=1;typ=item.get('type');mid=item.get('id');body=textof(item)
            role='user' if typ=='userMessage' else 'assistant' if typ=='agentMessage' else typ
            ref=body.startswith('::chatgpt-content-reference')
            truncated='"truncated": true' in json.dumps(item)
            tool_trunc+=int(truncated)
            if role=='assistant':
                if ref:assistant_refs+=1
                else:assistant_text+=1
            ptr='/turns/'+str(n-1)+'/items/'+str(j)
            anchor='message-'+str(mid)
            md+=['<a id="'+anchor+'"></a>','#### '+str(role)+' — '+str(mid),'']
            if ref:md+=['**Archive notice: source returned a reference placeholder; the assistant reply body is absent from this retrieval record.**','']
            if truncated:md+=['**Archive notice: retrieval explicitly flags this message or content part as truncated.**','']
            md+=[fence(body)]
            if typ not in ('userMessage','agentMessage'):md+=[fence(json.dumps(item,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),'json')]
            bu=browser_byid.get(mid)
            supplemented=bool(bu and meaningful_browser(bu) and (ref or truncated))
            if supplemented:
                browser_supplements+=1
                md+=['**Additional directly captured browser view of this same message.** This is rendered UI text, not verified original Markdown; UI labels may occur. The unedited DOM/HTML source is retained separately.','',fence(bu['renderedText'])]
            msgindex.append({'conversation_id':cid,'message_id':mid,'role':role,'turn_id':t.get('id'),
                'turn_started_at_raw':start,'turn_started_at_utc':iso(start),
                'timestamp_basis':'associated turn.startedAt; message creation timestamp unavailable',
                'retrieval_body_status':'reference_only' if ref else 'truncated' if truncated else 'available_text',
                'browser_supplement':supplemented,'browser_body_present':bool(bu and meaningful_browser(bu)),
                'json_locator':stem.with_suffix('.json').relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()+'#'+ptr,
